**Gate P4**: no field is required-but-undeclared, and no shipped resource kind is unauthorable.
**Status 2026-09-11: PASSED.** Both clauses are closed and each is held by a gate rather than by review. First clause — `validate:declared-surface` reports 36 loader keys across 3 resource types authorable or exempt, and the `gate_type` exemption that stood for the homonym is DELETED because the homonym is gone (P4.10). Second clause — `category` was the last shipped resource kind without a writer, and P4.7 gave it the full surface the owner ruled for, with zero new exemptions. **Sequence**: batch A closed the declaration rows; batch B killed P4.8 and corrected P4.2's premise; batch C (2026-09-07) closed P4.3 and P4.2 and killed P4.6, opening P4.11/P4.12 from the kill's fallout; dispatch batch 1 (2026-09-09) closed P4.11 and opened P4.13; batch 2 (2026-09-11) closed P4.9, P4.10 and P4.12; batch 3 closed P4.7 and P4.13. Batch 4 (2026-09-11) closed P4.14 and P4.15 and opened P4.16 through P4.21; batch 5 (2026-09-13) closed seven rows — P4.16, P4.17, P4.18, P4.19, P4.20, P4.21, P4.22 — and opened six from their measurements: P4.23 through P4.28. **Thirty of thirty-eight rows are terminal (twenty-eight ✓, two ✗); eight stay open.** Counted mechanically, not by hand — see below.

**That tally was wrong for at least two batches and the correction is worth more than the number.** This paragraph read "Thirteen of twenty-one rows are terminal (eleven ✓, two ✗)" on 2026-09-11, and batch 5 propagated its arithmetic forward before checking it. Counted mechanically on 2026-09-13 — `awk 'match($0, /^\| (P4\.[0-9]+) +\| +([✓✗☐⚠])/, m)'` over this file — the pre-batch-5 state was **thirteen ✓**, not eleven, so the plan understated its own completed work by two rows across every reading since. A hand-maintained count beside a table that can be counted is an untrusted inventory in the plan's own house, and this plan already carries a rule about re-measuring inventories before executing them.

**Eight rows stay open (P4.29, the test audit, joined 2026-09-13; P4.30, from the merge with main, joined 2026-09-15), none bears on the gate's clauses, and closing seven opened six — which is the honest shape of this work rather than a failure of it.** Batch 4 closed two rows and opened six; batch 5 closed seven of those and opened six more. The open set has turned over completely and its SIZE has barely moved, and that is the finding the count alone hides: each closure is a real property now held by a test, and each new row is something that property's own measurement exposed. P4.23 is the seventh shadow surface, found only because P4.18 enumerated the other six. P4.24 is the root P4.19's premise did not cover. P4.25 is the hot-reload path silently reversing three closed rows while a comment claims it does not. P4.26 is the gate that was supposed to hold the wiring and holds half of it. P4.27 and P4.28 are the two-derivations disagreement in the kinds and the direction P4.21 did not reach. **Every one exists because something was measured, not because something was deferred**, and each carries its own falsifier so none depends on this paragraph being re-read. Two of them — P4.25 and P4.26 — bear on rows already marked ✓, and they are open rows rather than reopened ones on purpose: the ✓ claims the property was observed, which it was; these say a channel exists where it is not preserved. The gate's two clauses are held by `validate:declared-surface` and by P4.7's writer, and neither is touched by any open row.

### Rulings R18–R22 (final slice, 2026-09-16)

Ruled BEFORE dispatch, because a question ruled after dispatch is N workers guessing N ways.

- **R18 (P4.33) — `tools/` is RESERVED; nothing under it is a prompt.** The indexer is right and the
  loader must stop recursing. P4.28 matched the loader on the grounds that the loader defines what is
  served, which was the correct tiebreak for THAT row and the wrong rule here: `tools/` already has a
  meaning (script tools) and its own composite id scheme (`{promptId}/{toolId}`), so admitting prompts
  there gives one directory two id schemes. `shared/utils/prompt-layout.ts` already documents `tools/`
  as reserved — the fix makes the loader match the rule the shared module states.
- **R19 (P4.37) — DELETE the read API with its tests.** Re-measured on `f2e07a45`: the only method
  reached on a `ResourceIndexer` instance anywhere in `src/` is `syncAll()`, at 2 call sites. Python
  hooks read `resource_index` with their own SQL and `skills-sync` runs a raw `SELECT`, so nothing
  wants an in-process ranked search. Keeping ~120 lines and ~26 tests alive for no consumer is the
  parallel-system shape this plan exists to remove. A future consumer writes the query it needs.
- **R20 (P4.42) — decompose `shutdown()`, NOT the file.** `refactoring.md` is explicit that splitting a
  file to satisfy a number produces a file whose only justification is arithmetic. Cognitive complexity
  57 against a limit of 15 is a real reading cost, and decomposing it drops both signals at once.
- **R21 (P4.41) — ONE shared staleness helper, not a per-file check.** 10+ e2e files spawn
  `dist/index.js`. `scripts/verify-mcp-surface.mjs` already has a working, documented `newestMtime`
  refusal — reuse it rather than re-derive it. A per-file guard is the 1-of-10 shape this arc has
  already paid for twice.
- **R22 (P4.31) — ONE `StyleDefinitionLoader` instance: the one `module-initializer` wires.**
  `StyleManager` building its own via `createStyleDefinitionLoader` (`style-manager.ts:95`) is the
  defect, not a second legitimate owner. Re-measured: `module-initializer.ts:417` and
  `tool-description-overlays.ts:118` both take the singleton; `StyleManager` is the lone dissenter.

**Two premises corrected on re-measurement (2026-09-16, `f2e07a45`):**

- **P4.32 said the shared layout module "has two remaining non-consumers".** It has none — all three
  walks (`resource-indexer.ts`, `resource-change-tracking.ts`, `yaml-prompt-loader.ts`) import it. What
  survives is narrower: the indexer keeps its OWN `entry.name === 'tools'` skip at line 1013 while the
  shared module documents the same rule in prose. The row is a duplicated RULE, not a missing import.
- **P4.38 is confirmed and slightly worse than written.** `SHARED` is guarded at line 537
  (`toBeGreaterThan(0)`); the `isolated` describe at line 559 has no such case at all, so its
  `it.each` at 589 generates zero tests silently.

### Rulings R25–R30 (open-rows slice, 2026-09-16)

Ruled before dispatching P4.43–P4.50, on `914b068c`. R23 and R24 stand as written in P4.47.

- **R25 (P4.47 + P4.48) — one worker, P4.47 first.** Both rows edit `resource-indexer.ts` and
  `validate-prompts.ts`, and P4.48's check would fail `validate-prompts.ts` until P4.47(c) makes it use
  the shared predicates. The four walks that are only shielded by depth ADOPT the shared predicates.
  Depth is not an exception. The one declared exception is `cli/src/lib/workspace.ts`, which is a
  separate package. The exception is anchored by path and fails closed when that file disappears. The
  three "is this a category" copies are measured, not fixed. If they disagree, that is a new row.
- **R26 (P4.49) — the loader returns the validator's output, defaults included.** Styles have no
  write path (`resource_type` has no `style` member), so no read-back can write a defaulted value to
  disk as if it were authored. The worker lists every reader of a loaded style. If any reader needs
  the authored-only form, the worker stops and returns that as a concern.
- **R27 (P4.43) — `verify-unknown-interrupt.mjs` imports `checkDistFreshness`, and a check owns the
  class.** The check keys on shape: a script that reads an mtime under `dist` and under `src`
  without importing `dist-freshness.js`. `dist-freshness.js` itself is the one exemption, named by
  path.
- **R28 (P4.44) — report the failure, keep the control flow.** The inner catch stays, because it is
  what guarantees the database close. Each teardown failure also reaches stderr directly, on the
  channel the server's other operator messages use, so it is visible under STDIO. The exit code does
  not change in this row.
- **R29 (P4.45 + P4.46) — (c) always; (b) as a bounded attempt.** The worker states knip's scope beside
  `validate:knip-ratchet`. It then tries (b) as a ts-morph reference check over public methods of
  classes in `src/`, ratcheted like knip, with a 90 s runtime budget. The positive control is that
  the check names `getValidStyles` and `getValidFrameworks` BEFORE P4.46 deletes them. If the check
  cannot meet the budget, or its false positives cannot be baselined, the worker ships (c) plus the
  deletions and returns (b) as a concern. The planner then kills (b) with the measured reason.
- **R30 (P4.50) — the check lives in `validate-plan-row-tracking.js`.** It is the gate that already
  owns plan tables, so no new script. A table row that follows a blank line and has no
  header+separator pair of its own is a break. The error names the plan, the line and the first
  dropped row. It is verified against this file with the `6d8da146` break restored.
- **R31 (P4.53, ruled mid-slice) — one category-directory predicate in `prompt-layout.ts`.** It
  excludes `.`/`_`-prefixed names, `backup` and `node_modules`. Every depth-0 decision uses it,
  including the loader's own copies, so the loader stops serving a `node_modules` category. cli mirrors
  the rule under the P4.48 exception, which is extended rather than duplicated.
- **R31a (re-ruled on the P4.53 handoff) — cli is not an exception.** R31 and P4.48 both said that cli
  cannot import server source. That is false: `cli/tsconfig.json` and `cli/esbuild.config.mjs` both
  map `@shared` to `server/src/shared`, and cli already imports from it. Neither premise was probed.
  cli imports the shared predicates, and its entry in the EXCEPTIONS table is deleted.
- **R32 (P3.1–P3.3, owner 2026-09-17) — killed.** Styles stay file-authored.
- **R33 (P5.12, owner 2026-09-17) — rename `strategicImplement` → `strategic_implement` and
  `diagnosisCard` → `diagnosis_card` across this repo, `~/.claude`, `opencode-prompts` and
  `gemini-prompts`, with no alias.**
  - **A prompt id and its exported skill name stay ONE identifier (owner: "they should just have
    the same id").** The Agent Skills spec says a `name` is lowercase letters, digits and hyphens
    only. Measured tolerance: Claude Code already loads `strategic_worker`, and OpenCode's code has
    no name regex (anomalyco/opencode#9294). Accepted deviation; it flips if a client starts
    rejecting underscores.
  - **Plans are exempt, like CHANGELOG.** References inside `plans/**` are records.
  - The rename is breaking and gets a CHANGELOG breaking entry.
  - P5.13 follows once no camelCase id remains.
- **R34 (P5.16, owner 2026-09-17) — rename both.** `content_processing` → `content-processing` and
  `dev-workflow` → `dev_workflow`, through `resource_manager`. The exported skill therefore becomes
  `/dev_workflow`, and about 51 references in `~/.claude` move with it.
- **R35 (P4.55) — widen `ResourceEntry` to carry its entry file.** Every cpm consumer reads the
  file, not `join(dir, entryFile)`. `delete` removes a single-file prompt's file, never its
  category. `rename` works on a nested step. Ids are the loader's composite ids.
- **R36 (P4.52, owner 2026-09-17) — burn down by module.** At most 8 batches per slice. A method
  kept on purpose gets a stated reason recorded beside its baseline entry.
- **R37 (P4.73, planner 2026-09-20) — emit where the value is already in scope, else remove.** An
  attribute whose value is available where the pipeline root span's other attributes are set is
  emitted there; one that needs new plumbing is removed from the type, the allowlist and the guide.
  The row closes with a check that fails on an allowlisted attribute with no emitter.
- **R38 (P4.65, planner 2026-09-20) — `edges` becomes authorable; nothing is pruned silently.**
  `resource_manager` accepts `edges` on a chain prompt's create and update, along the path
  `chainSteps` travels. The refusal stays and names the remedy. Additive to the tool contract.
- **R39 (P4.68, planner 2026-09-20) — fix the producer, then the key, then the restore.** A rename
  or move onto an id with history renumbers the incoming rows after the target's highest version.
  A unique index on `(tenant_id, resource_type, resource_id, version)` ships with `SCHEMA_VERSION` 28. `ensureSchema()` renumbers colliding rows on restore instead of throwing or dropping them.
- **R40 (P4.64, owner 2026-09-20) — adopt the `yaml` Document API.** A resource write keeps the
  file's comments and the layout of keys it did not change. The dependency lands in its own PR from
  a worktree with a real install, because every other worktree shares one `node_modules`.
- **R41 (P4.71, owner 2026-09-20) — wire a consumer that benefits.** Standardize the shape where
  other systems need it. A discovery row ranks the consumers and enumerates every verdict shape
  that is rendered to text and parsed back, before any implementation row is cut.
- **R42 (restored methods, owner 2026-09-20) — suspect a lost caller before deleting.**
  `hasActiveVerification`, `restoreFromBackup` and `VersionHistoryService.rollback` /
  `.deleteHistory` stay out of the P4.52 batches until a history audit says whether each lost its
  caller, was never wired, or is surplus.
- **R43 (P4.68, planner 2026-09-20) — a version save is one locked read-then-write.** Both writers.
- **R45 (P4.68, planner 2026-09-20) — the server connection waits like the CLI's.** One shared
  `busy_timeout` constant, so the two cannot drift.
- **R44 (P4.80, planner 2026-09-20) — wire the six chain and framework emitters, do not delete.**
- **R46 (P4.84, planner 2026-09-20, owner to confirm) — refuse an undeclared key by name.** It
  follows #337, which the owner merged as breaking; it lands in the same unreleased major.
- **R47 (P4.52, planner 2026-09-20) — a restored method is made reached, never baselined upward.**
  Its live duplicates call it, where a test first proves each duplicate equivalent.
- **R48 (P4.52, planner 2026-09-20) — search for the question before deleting.** The baseline
  already says nothing references the name, and a reimplementation never carries it. Per method:
  state its job in one sentence, then search for anything else that answers it (literal lists,
  private helpers, duplicated constants). A hit keeps the method and opens a row naming both sites.
  Two batches found ten such methods this way; a name search found none of them.
- **R29 follow-ups (ruled on the P4.45 handoff).** A method called only from tests counts as
  unreached. A stale baseline entry fails the check.

### Findings (P4 batch B)

- **P4-F32 — a gate that fires on the right file for the wrong reason is the defect it was built to
  catch, recursing.** P4.36's brief specified a PREDICATE, not a property: _"a docblock naming a
  `src/` file or symbol as real must import it."_ The worker built it, measured it on the live tree
  first, and got **8 findings, all false alarms**, of three kinds — `create<Subject>()` factories
  (`createParsingSystem` standing in for `UnifiedCommandParser`), transitive reach through a
  constructed collaborator (`ResourceVerificationService` behind `FileOperations`), and one header
  _disclaiming_ coverage (_"a unit test of `PathResolver` cannot show that…"_) read as a claim.
  **Absence of an import is not absence of reach.** Worse, the predicate would have caught the
  `startup-bootstrap` instance for the WRONG reason: it fires on `ResourceIndexer`, which that file
  genuinely reached via `createResourceIndexer` — the actual lie is the words _"at startup"_, which
  name no symbol at all. The shipped gate reads two decidable properties instead: a subject bound
  only by `import type` (erased before the test runs) and an `@lifecycle` line claiming startup from
  a file that value-imports nothing under `src/runtime/` and spawns no process. **The rule for
  briefs: specify the property and a required false-positive count on the live tree, not the
  predicate.** "It must catch its own motivating instances" needs the companion clause _for the
  right reason_ — otherwise the gate's own green is a true assertion under a false header.

- **P4-F31 — mutation-verifying a REFUSAL needs every other guard on the path shown not to fire.** A
  refusal test is satisfied by ANY refusal, so when the code under test validates later, the case
  passes against the unfixed source for the wrong reason. P4.24's first falsifier did exactly that:
  framework creation requires `system_prompt_guidance`, `phases` and `framework_gates`, and a
  minimal `{action, id, name}` create is refused by validation whether or not the new guard exists.
  The rule: when the falsifier is "X is refused", assert WHICH refusal (`toContain('failed to
load')`, never bare `isError`) and make every other guard on the path pass. This is the negative
  half of the positive-control rule, and this plan has now been bitten by it once per polarity.

- **P4-F30 — a deliberate non-ownership clause in a canonical module is an open row written in
  prose.** `prompt-layout.ts` documented, with reasoning, "WHAT IT DELIBERATELY DOES NOT DECIDE:
  where in the tree a file may sit" — and the unshared half is precisely where the next defect
  (P4.28) lived. The stated cost of owning it, "would have to be handed a root", turned out to be one
  extra parameter. Same shape from a second direction: `checkFrameworkExists` calls itself a
  "Comprehensive existence check across all framework state sources" and enumerates three, none of
  which is the disk beyond one root — and P4.19 cited that comment as its reason not to fix
  frameworks, which is how P4.24 came to exist at all. **A completeness claim in a comment is
  unfalsifiable unless it names its enumeration**, and a reasoned refusal to own something should
  carry an as-of date and a falsifier exactly as a `☐` does. `cleanup-standards.md` already says a
  documented blind spot is the sharpest case because it reads as rigour; these are its second and
  third sightings in this plan.

- **P4-F29 — a gate on a property's PRESENCE cannot see what the property carries, and optionality is
  why.** `quarantine` is optional by design, which is exactly what made presence the only checkable
  thing — and the same optionality is why a PARTIAL view typechecks and every suite stays green,
  since the consumer suites build their own view and never observe production's wiring. P4.26's
  answer: resolve the expression behind the property through local bindings, nested
  `mergeQuarantineViews(...)` calls and one parameter hop, have each consumer declare the coverage
  its own walk requires, and — because the complete view crosses four files — carry it under one
  declared name whose every write in `src/` is checked, so a reference is trusted only because its
  writes were. This is the successor to "a validator with no call site reads as coverage": **a call
  site with a partial argument reads as a wired one.** A strictly stronger fix exists and is not
  taken: a `buildCatalogQuarantineView({prompt, gate, framework, style})` returning a branded type,
  with the indexer's config typed to the brand, would make the COMPILER enforce completeness and
  shrink this gate to almost nothing — worth a row if the shape recurs.

- **P4-F28 — for a single-file resource the write projection and the snapshot diff COINCIDE, so the
  obvious control passes without the fix.** P4.30's first proving case — seed a category, update it,
  roll it back — was green against the unfixed source, and the reason generalizes. A category's
  authored content is one file, and the snapshot's canonical key order happens to equal
  `buildCategoryYaml`'s, with both sides serializing through the same `serializeYaml`. So whenever
  the file on disk was last written BY THE WRITER, the two agree byte-for-byte. Gates and frameworks
  diverge unconditionally only because their content spans two files. The divergence for a
  single-file kind appears exclusively against a document the writer did not produce — a comment, a
  different key order, an unfolded long scalar. The case now rolls back straight off a hand-authored
  fixture with no tool write in between, which is what makes it fail without the fix, and that is
  also where P4.7 categories actually live: hand-authored `category.yaml`. **The trap for the next
  reader**: "simplifying" that fixture by writing it through the writer kills the assertion silently.
  Second sighting of the shape the byte-identity work already recorded — a control seeded by the
  code under test cannot discriminate.

- **P4-F27 — a collection can be correctly wired and permanently empty, and every gate and every
  test will pass.** P4.16's brief (mine) said to expose the style quarantine through `StyleManager`
  and merge it at the composition root. Both halves were wrong, and neither would have failed
  anything. `StyleManager` is not used by the composition root at all — it is built only by
  `PromptExecutor`, over its own loader and its own root set, and `isRefused` is path-keyed, so a
  record from that instance can never match a path the indexer walks. Worse: **nothing loaded a
  style at startup.** The inventory line called `discoverStyles()`, which resolves through
  `discoverYamlDirectories` → `existsSync` and never opens a file, so the sink would have stayed
  empty however broken the tree was. Verified in the owning session both ways: the helper's body is
  an `existsSync` filter, and the fix is `loadAllStyles().size` — a startup-behaviour change an
  operator sees. **What makes this the sharpest instance in this plan**: the tests could not have
  caught it, because they call `loadAllStyles()` themselves, so the suite exercises a code path the
  product never reaches. `refactoring.md`'s `reached` probe asks whether the new path RUNS rather
  than compiles, and it is the only check in the pre-flight that would have fired here.

- **P4-F26 — a gate can be blind to the primitive its own subject is built on, and satisfying it
  then means being invisible to it.** I told P4.20's executor to place its version record so
  `validate:mutation-atomicity` accepts it, and not to edit the gate. Both halves of that
  instruction could not be obeyed at once: `RECORDING_METHODS` was `['recordEditResult',
'commitEdit']`, and R9 directs the repair at `saveVersion` — the primitive the other two are
  built ON. **Measured in the owning session, which is what settles it**: with the pre-row list the
  gate reports `✅ 8 version record(s)` on the finished tree and does not see the two new sites at
  all; with `saveVersion` added it reports 10. So the durable write this row exists to add would
  have passed a gate that could not observe it. The list was complete only by accident — no call
  site under `src/mcp/tools/` used `saveVersion` before this row. Widening it is strictly stricter
  and relaxes nothing, and the executor was right to override the instruction and say so. Verified
  both directions: renaming the `commit:` property to `afterWrite:` makes the gate name
  `gate-lifecycle-processor.ts:346` exactly. **The reusable part is the shape of my error** — "make
  the existing gate pass" is only a safe instruction when the gate's scope is known to cover the
  new construct, and a gate whose subject is a method NAME can never be assumed to.

- **P4-F25 — a test suite's own stub config can make a LATER row's falsifier vacuous, and nothing
  connects the two.** P4.15's repair suite was written with `getVersioningConfig: () => ({ enabled:
false, … })` and no `DatabasePort` — correct for P4.15, which asserts nothing about versions. But
  P4.20's falsifier is a row COUNT in `version_history`, so had it been written against that suite
  as configured, `toBe(1)` would have been measured in a process where versioning could not write
  at all. P4.20's executor found it and switched the shared stub to enabled plus a real
  `SqliteEngine` per suite. Nothing in the row, the plan or the gates would have caught this: the
  disabling lives in a fixture two rows away, and a vacuous green looks exactly like a real one.
  Same family as the positive-control rule, one level up — the control has to fire in the
  configuration the assertion actually runs in.

- **P4-F24 — a rendering can be dead rather than merely wrong, and only a mutation tells them
  apart.** P4.18 was authored (by me, in the brief) as "both surfaces render 'served from another
  root'". Three of the four renderings did. The framework `list` rendered **no shadow line at all,
  for any framework, ever**: `framework-manager.ts:634` sets `id: guide.frameworkId.toUpperCase()`
  on every served projection while a quarantine record's id is the lower-cased directory name, so
  `summarizeQuarantine`'s map could never pair them. The tell is the mutation profile — removing
  the id normalization fails ONLY the new assertion and leaves every pre-existing test in the suite
  green, which is what a surface with no coverage looks like from the outside. Re-run in the owning
  session: 1 failed / 20 passed. Gates carried the same gap latently, because `validateGateSchema`
  compares declared id to directory name case-insensitively, so `id: Case-Gate` under `case-gate/`
  loads and never matches its own record.

- **P4-F23 — `lint:ratchet` passing is not evidence of zero added debt, and two independent
  executors hit that in one batch.** The committed baseline is a CEILING (3200 errors / 1020
  warnings) against a measured actual of 3102 / 976 — roughly 100 errors and 44 warnings of slack.
  P4.14's executor pushed `compareBaseline` from ≤15 to cognitive complexity **16** and the ratchet
  reported "no regressions"; P4.15's executor landed at 3105 / 980 and also passed. Both found it
  only by measuring `HEAD` separately and diffing per rule, and both then fixed rather than consumed
  the slack. Two sightings in one batch, plus the pre-existing memory note, is the argument for
  treating a ratchet PASS as "no regression detected" rather than "no debt added" in any receipt
  this plan writes. Related: `max-lines` on `application.ts` sat at 998 and blocked at +6 code
  lines, which is what produced `reportSyncFindings` — a real consolidation, arrived at by a
  constraint rather than by taste, and recorded as such.
- **P4-F22 — the wiring that makes P4.14 true is invisible to every gate P4.14 passed.**
  `quarantine` is an OPTIONAL config field, deliberately, so an indexer built without one indexes
  every parseable file. The cost is that DELETING the wiring at either production call site
  typechecks, passes both ratchets, passes `validate:all`, and passes every unit, integration and
  e2e test — because the consumer suites construct their own view and never observe whether
  production reaches it. The row's tests are thorough about behaviour and structurally cannot see
  this. Closed by `validate:refusal-aware-consumers` (TS AST, 4 self-test shapes plus the live
  tree), with all three real call sites individually unwired as positive controls. **Two of those
  three controls silently failed to mutate on the first attempt** — one regex missed because the
  property is not shorthand, one matched a type annotation instead of the config — and each reported
  a false green. A control that does not mutate is indistinguishable from a gate that works, which
  is the second half of this finding and the more general half.
- **P4-F21 — "the collection is written and proven; what remains is the two sibling loaders" was
  wrong three times over, and each correction changed the design.** (1) `beginRoot` CLEARS a whole
  root, which describes `PromptLoader` exactly — it walks a root inside one method — and describes
  neither sibling: gates and frameworks resolve ONE id at a time behind a cache, driven by a
  registry loop at startup and a single-id `reload` after. Clearing a root on either would erase
  every other broken file's record, so the collection gained a non-clearing `sinkFor` and a
  per-file `forget`. Without `forget` a successful repair still reports "still quarantined" — a
  mutation confirms it, killing three tests including the falsifier. (2) The brief said to bind the
  view at `mcp/tools/index.ts` the way the prompt handler does; that is wrong, because
  `FrameworkToolHandler` is constructed inside `setFrameworkManager()`, before which the registry
  may not exist — a view bound there is the empty stand-in for the process's life. The processors
  ask their manager on each call instead. (3) The refusal sites are all in
  `gate-definition-loader.ts`; `gate-loader.ts` only observes the resulting `undefined`. **A row
  that inherits a mechanism inherits the mechanism's SHAPE, not just its code**, and the shape was
  fitted to one loader's unit of work.
- **P4-F20 — a component justified by a mechanism it is not part of, in this plan's own hands.**
  The P4.14/P4.15 groundwork added `type` to the quarantine's map key, with the stated reason that
  `beginRoot` clears and a gate walk would otherwise erase a root's prompt records. The erasure is
  real; the attribution was false — `forgetRoot` filters on the record's fields, so the key never
  participates. Removing `type` from the key killed NO test; removing it from the filter killed one
  and from the record stamp killed two. The claim survived writing, review and a green suite, and
  was caught only by running the mutation the receipt implied. Corrected by superseding rather than
  rewriting (`3f085048`), because the shape is the reusable part: an inert component with a
  confident reason reads more deliberate than a correct one with none.
- **P4-F5 — P4.2 named one processor and the defect had three.** `recordEditResult`-before-write
  was identical in the gate and prompt processors. The prompt side had even reasoned about the
  ordering in a long comment (P7-D2) — and reasoned about the OTHER direction, a failed snapshot
  followed by a successful write, which it prevented correctly. Guarding one direction of a
  two-directional failure reads, in review and in the comment, exactly like guarding the failure.
- **P4-F6 — a comment claimed the coverage that the code's nesting denied.**
  `framework-lifecycle-processor.ts:264` states the bundled-tree guard "also covers `focus`,
  `liquescent`, `radiant` and `verify` ... (P4.3)". That guard is nested inside
  `if (!existsSync(frameworkDir))`, so it is reachable only when the directory is ABSENT — which
  is never true for the four ids it names. The claim cites the plan row by number, which is what
  makes it read as discharged work. A citation is not a test.
- **P4-F7 — a killed capability left its row alive, and the row asked for the capability back.**
  P4.8's falsifier was written against a feature that was later removed on purpose. Because `☐`
  asserts nothing, nothing re-read it, and executing the row as written would have reintroduced
  author-controlled file paths into a guided authoring workflow. This is the `cleanup-standards.md`
  §A Status Outlives What It Described asymmetry with teeth: the stale `☐` did not merely waste a
  pass, it pointed at a security regression.
- **P4-F9 — SF-3 and an earlier safety property of this same plan are in direct conflict, and
  only the integration suite said so.** Record-before-write leaves a version row for a write that
  never landed; record-after-write leaves a written file with no version row. An earlier row here
  established and TESTED the second as the unacceptable one
  (`gate-framework-versioning.integration.test.ts`, "a persistence failure leaves the file
  unmodified"), reasoning that a state recorded nowhere cannot be rolled back to. P4.2 was written
  later, from the other direction, and neither row cites the other. Reordering was implemented,
  passed typecheck, the ratchet, `validate:all` 55/55 and the entire 3026-test unit suite — and
  was caught by ONE integration test. The unit suites could not see it: the property is about a
  cross-medium failure ordering, and every unit harness stubs one of the two media. This is the
  `test:ci`-is-not-`test:all` hazard the project handbook already records, arriving through a
  door the handbook does not name — the change was not under-tested, it was tested at the wrong
  altitude.
- **P4-F10 — "strictly better" was my own unmeasured claim, and it was wrong.** I justified the
  reorder by arguing a missing version row is milder than a phantom one. That argument is
  defensible in isolation and I did not check whether the repository had already ruled on it. It
  had, in a comment attached to the test that later failed. A prior decision recorded next to its
  own test is not findable by reading the code being changed — which is the argument for running
  the full suite BEFORE reasoning about a trade-off, not after.
- **P4-F18 — a `✓` row cited a gate by a name that does not exist, and cited it three times.**
  P1.7's evidence, P1.8's receipt and P4.9's constraint 3 all named
  `validate:contained-resource-writes`. There is no such script; the gate is
  `validate:resource-path-containment`. Every OTHER `validate:*` name the plan cites resolves in
  `package.json` — enumerated, not spot-checked — so this is one wrong name repeated, not sloppy
  citation generally. It matters because of how it fails: a reader verifying P1.7 runs the named
  command and gets `Missing script`, which reads as broken tooling rather than a wrong citation, so
  the natural response is to distrust the gate instead of the plan. **A cited command is a claim
  that can be executed, which makes it the cheapest kind of claim to check and the easiest to leave
  unchecked.** Corrected in all four places including the notes file; the enumeration that found it
  is two lines of shell and belongs in whatever eventually gates plan prose.
- **P4-F19 — the row's premise understated the defect, and the true behaviour was the opposite
  shape.** P4.9 said `update` answers `Prompt not found` for an unloadable file. Measured against
  `dist/` at `56138c35`, `inspect` does — but `update` with a full body answers
  `✅ **Prompt Updated**`, writes a SECOND prompt at `general/<id>` via the fallback category,
  prints `Moved prompt 'x' from 'x' to 'general'` when nothing moved, and leaves the broken file
  untouched. So the pre-state was **a silent duplicate under a success headline**, not a refusal.
  The row was written from the `inspect` symptom and generalised to the tool. A refusal is loud and
  costs a repair path; a success that writes the wrong file costs a repair path AND leaves a second
  resource nobody asked for. **Reading one action's behaviour and attributing it to the surface is
  how a row understates its own defect** — and the fix is the same either way, which is exactly why
  nobody would have caught it without driving the real tool.

- **P4-F16 — `format` had a FOURTH declaration site, and only the typechecker knew.** P4.12 named
  three: the schema, the contract, and `_generated`. Removing them broke `typecheck` on
  `src/mcp/metadata/definitions/prompt-resource.ts`, which carried `'format'` in
  `PROMPT_RESOURCE_PARAMETER_NAMES` typed against the generated union — a declaration site, not a
  reader, which is exactly why the row's reader-hunting probe could not see it. **The probe that
  found the phantom was the wrong probe for enumerating it**: "who reads this" and "where is this
  declared" are different questions, and the row answered the first while its work item required
  the second. A FIFTH remains and was deliberately left: `BasePromptResourceArgs.format` in
  `tools/types/shared-types.ts`, a different enum whose whole `PromptResourceAction` union has zero
  consumers outside its own file — a dead-type finding, not this row's.
- **P4-F17 — the rename broke a bundled prompt's tool, and nothing in the plan would have caught
  it.** `create_gate`'s `gate_builder/script.py` built its payload as `"gate_type": data["type"]`.
  After P4.10 that sends `"validation"` into an enum of `framework|category|custom`. Proved against
  the built schema rather than argued: the pre-rename payload now `safeParse`s to `false`, the
  post-rename one to `true`. **A tool-surface rename has consumers inside the repository's own
  resource tree**, and no gate walks `resources/**/tools/*.py` for tool-parameter names. The
  `create_gate` prompt's own ARGUMENT is still spelled `gate_type` and was deliberately not renamed
  — it is a prompt argument, the same prompt already declares a separate `type` argument, and
  merging them would change that prompt's interface. Confusing one layer up, and flagged rather
  than fixed.

- **P4-F15 — the orphan-row defect recurred in P6 two days after it was "fixed" in P4, exactly as
  P4-F14 predicted.** Writing the P6 audit back, `planRowStates` parsed P6.1–P6.3 and silently
  dropped P6.4 and P6.5 — the same blank-line-before-an-appended-row shape, in a different phase of
  the same file. P4-F14 closed the four instances it found and named the durable fix in prose:
  "a parser that silently drops what it cannot classify reports coverage it does not have."
  **Naming the fix is not making it** — and the repair itself repeated the mistake it described.
  P4-F14 fixed the four rows it had tripped over and stopped there. Only when this recurrence
  forced a full enumeration did the real number appear: **fifteen orphan rows across P4, P5 and
  P6**, eleven of them still present after the "fix". The file parsed **43 rows and contains 66**.
  P5 was showing 6 of its 17 rows; P6, 3 of 5. So P4-F14 both stated the class rule and closed 4 of
  15 instances, in the same breath.

  This is `dev-workflow.md` §"a fix at the sites you found is not a fix of the class", caught by its
  own rule on a two-day cycle — and the shortest possible demonstration of why that rule exists.
  **The closure condition is a gate, not another repair**: `validate:plan-row-tracking` should fail
  when a line matching `| P<n>.<m> |` is not inside a parsed table, because that line is either a
  row the parser must see or a formatting error, and neither is a thing to skip. Until that exists,
  every future phase of every plan carries the same silent hole. Filed as row **P5.17** so it is
  work rather than narration.

- **P4-F14 — four rows were invisible to the parser that grades whether a PR closes a row.**
  _(Heading corrected 2026-09-09 in the PR that closes P4.11. It first read "invisible to every plan
  gate, and the gates reported green over them", which the body of this same finding had already
  withdrawn as unmeasured — the scoped claim was written into the body and the overclaiming heading
  was left standing above it. A summary line is a claim like any other, and it is the one most
  readers stop at.)_
  A blank line sat between each of P4.9, P4.10, P4.11 and P4.12, so each was an orphan table row
  rather than part of the P4 table. Markdown needs a header row to make a table; the parser
  `planRowStates` — which `validate-pr-body`'s `Plan:` footer check imports — saw **43 rows where
  there are 47**, measured against both `origin/main` and the repaired file. The four it dropped
  were exactly P4.9 through P4.12. The consequence: that check grades whether a PR closes a row by
  diffing row states between the merge base and the head, so **no PR could ever have been credited
  with closing any of those four rows**, and the failure would have read as "this PR closes none of
  them" — an accusation about the PR rather than a report about the parser. Measured on PR #273,
  which does close P4.11.

  Scope of the claim, deliberately narrow: this is about the row-state parser. Whether
  `validate:plan-row-tracking`'s stamp count was also affected is NOT established — the repair left
  its reported total unchanged at 39, and `auditOpenRows` returns `graded: false` for this file
  against both revisions, which is a separate question this row does not answer and should not be
  read as answering.

  Two things make this worse than a formatting slip. It is _invisible in rendered form_ — GitHub
  shows the orphan rows as ordinary text lines that look close enough to a table to pass a reading
  eye. And the rows arrived one at a time, each appended after a blank line, so no single edit
  looked wrong. **A parser that silently drops what it cannot classify reports coverage it does not
  have**; the durable fix is that dropping a `| P<n>.<m> |`-shaped line should be a finding, not a
  skip.

- **P4-F13 — the anchor named a method that cannot be reached, and the reachable one would have
  lied.** Batch 1 was dispatched against `gate-loader.ts:290 toLightweightGate`, which does project
  `severity`/`enforcementMode` conditionally — and is `private` to `GateFileSystemLoader`, so no
  `inspect` path can call it. The object `inspect` actually holds resolves both fields to a loader
  default in its constructor, so a renderer reading `gate.severity` would have printed
  `Severity: medium` for a gate.yaml that says nothing, and a conformance scenario asserting
  `Severity: blocking` after creating with `severity: blocking` would still have PASSED. The
  vacuity R3 was written to prevent arrived through a different door: not a weak assertion, but a
  strong assertion against a field that is never absent. The general shape — **a conditional
  projection and an unconditional default can hold the same field name, and only the one you
  cannot reach preserves the distinction you are testing for.**
- **P4-F12 — two of the eleven fields had no read path at all, and the exception said the opposite.**
  The framework advanced-fields exception read "the router forwarded them and the writer split them
  across framework.yaml and phases.yaml throughout", implying the write side was complete and only
  the read side was missing. For `judge_prompt` and `execution_type_enhancements` that was false:
  the first is inlined by `loadExistingFramework` and dropped before `FrameworkCreationData`, the
  second is written to `phases.yaml` and never read by anything. Both were declared authorable by
  P4.1/P4.5 and verified by reading YAML in a test, which is exactly the proof P4.11 exists to
  replace — a YAML assertion cannot see that nothing will ever read the key back. **A round trip
  is a different measurement from a write check, and only the round trip found these.**
- **P4-F11 — a row miscounted its own closure set against an exception that had already refused
  it, in the same commit.** P4.11 claims three exceptions were repointed to it; two were, and the
  third says in its own text that it deliberately was not. Both edits landed together, so no drift
  over time explains it — the row was written from the intent ("repoint P4.6's exceptions") rather
  than from the result. The reusable half is the counting UNIT: "three exceptions" is unverifiable
  because `exceptionGroup` expands per parameter, so the same file supports 2, 3 or 13 depending on
  what you count. The gate's own total is the only count both sides can read, which is why the row's
  verification now names 67 → 54 instead of a number of groups. A closure condition should be
  phrased in whatever the gate prints.
- **P4-F8 — the obvious fix for P4.3 is wrong, and only measuring the two paths shows it.**
  `getBundledResourceDir` returns `<packageRoot>/resources/<type>`, which EQUALS
  `getFrameworksDirectory()` in a default install. So "refuse anything that exists in the bundled
  tree" would also refuse to delete a framework the operator created. The property the guard needs
  is "ships with the package", which no disk test can answer in that configuration.

## Execution Dispatch

Sequenced on FILE REGIONS, not on row size — see the preamble. `Agent` is the executor hint a
compiled node carries (`heavy`/`standard`/`fast` become `subagentModel`; `main thread` keeps the row
in the owning session).

| Row                   | Agent       | Batch | Why                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| --------------------- | ----------- | ----- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| ~~P4.11~~             | standard    | 1 ✓   | DONE 2026-09-09. Exceptions 67 → 50; three anchors were wrong (P4-F12, P4-F13)                                                                                                               |
| ~~P4.9~~              | heavy       | 2 ✓   | DONE 2026-09-11. Loader + registry; all four constraints held, M4 reproduced the predicted availability regression                                                                           |
| ~~P4.10+P4.12~~       | heavy       | 2 ✓   | DONE 2026-09-11. One change as predicted; the rename also broke a bundled prompt tool (P4-F17)                                                                                               |
| ~~P4.7~~              | main thread | 3 ✓   | DONE 2026-09-11. Full resource type; two defects fixed because the writer was unobservable without them                                                                                      |
| ~~P4.15~~             | heavy       | 4 ✓   | DONE 2026-09-11. Three brief premises wrong; the collection needed a real extension, not two sinks (P4-F21)                                                                                  |
| ~~P4.14~~             | heavy       | 4 ✓   | DONE 2026-09-11. Both consumers wired; the wiring needed its own gate, and the join a third commit (P4-F22)                                                                                  |
| ~~P4.16+17+21+22~~    | heavy       | 5 ✓   | DONE 2026-09-13. Four commits; the style collection would have shipped wired-and-empty (P4-F27), the accept rule needed five conditions not three, and the wiring is held by no gate (P4.26) |
| ~~P4.26→P4.25~~       | opus        | 6 ✓   | DONE 2026-09-15. The gate failed its motivating instance first. Opened P4.31: two style-loader instances, only one of them reloaded                                                          |
| ~~P4.28+P4.23+P4.24~~ | opus        | 6 ✓   | DONE 2026-09-15. The shared module grew to own position and id. Opened P4.32 (two non-consumers left) and P4.33 (`tools/` disagreement)                                                      |
| ~~P4.29~~             | opus        | 6 ✓   | DONE 2026-09-15. 41 paths censused. The recurring shape is a true assertion under a false header; execution is P4.36                                                                         |
| ~~P4.27~~             | opus        | 6 ✓   | DONE 2026-09-15. The order is stated once. Found a FOURTH derivation that omitted the primary (`prompt-executor.ts`), and opened P4.34/P4.35 for what the new order made untrue              |
| ~~P4.30~~             | opus        | 6 ✓   | DONE 2026-09-15. Cleared the branch's red. The proving case only discriminates against a hand-authored fixture (P4-F28)                                                                      |
| ~~P4.19+P4.20~~       | heavy       | 5 ✓   | DONE 2026-09-13. `create` refuses and the repair records version 1. The gate meant to police that placement was blind to it (P4-F26), and the row's premise held for one root of two (P4.24) |
| ~~P4.18~~             | heavy       | 5 ✓   | DONE 2026-09-13. Four renderings; one of them had never rendered at all (P4-F24), and enumerating them found a seventh surface (P4.23)                                                       |

**Batch 1 runs alone**, on purpose: it is the smallest row and it proves this dispatch section
compiles before any fan-out. **It did, and the run paid for itself in a way size did not predict**
(2026-09-09): the delegated node compiled and rendered, but three of the four anchors the owning
session had measured were wrong, and one of them — reading the field off the object `inspect`
actually holds — would have produced a renderer and a passing conformance scenario that both
reported a loader default as an authored value. A bounded row is not a low-information row. The
first executor also died mid-discovery to an API rate limit; the worktree measured byte-clean, so
relaunching cost nothing, which is the argument for measuring the tree rather than resuming blind. **Batch 2's two entries are genuinely parallel** — loader/registry
against router/contract, disjoint regions, so two worktrees. **Batch 3 is last** and that cost is
named rather than hidden: P4.7 is Gate P4's blocker, so the gate stays blocked until the end. The
alternative puts the largest and least-specified row ahead of everything and still collides with
batch 2.

**A delegated node's acceptance gate fires when the BRIEF renders, not when the work returns**
(measured 2026-09-09, batch 1). The executor runs outside the chain, so the chain has no way to
wait for it: attaching the row's falsifier as a gate on the delegated node asks for a verdict on a
diff that does not exist yet, and the only truthful answer is FAIL. Batch 1's run was cancelled for
exactly this. Batches 2 and 3 should carry a gate the DISPATCH can satisfy — worktree created,
rulings taken, brief self-contained — and leave the row's falsifier where it belongs, judged in the
owning session against the returned diff and written back to the row. A gate whose subject is not
reachable at the moment it is evaluated is the same defect `pr-conventions.yml` was fixed for on
2026-09-08, arriving from the other direction.

Every delegated batch takes its own worktree (`npm run worktree:create`, run from `server/`): a
subagent inherits the caller's HEAD, so two agents on one checkout fight over it. Check
`git config core.hooksPath` is still relative afterwards — anthropics/claude-code#72714 writes an
absolute path into the SHARED config and silently disables hooks everywhere.

### Ruling — P4.7 scope: full resource type (owner-ruled 2026-09-08)

`category` gets the same surface every other resource kind has — create, update, delete, inspect,
history — not the minimal `category.yaml` writer that would have flipped the falsifier most cheaply.

The falsifier is unchanged and stays deliberately narrow: a tool call writes a `category.yaml`. It
measures the MCP-Tooling-Only violation, which is what Gate P4's second clause is about; the wider
surface is what the owner ruled the row should DELIVER, not a wider thing for the gate to measure.
Recorded because the two can drift: a later session reading only the falsifier would build the
minimal writer and correctly report the row closed.

This is feature work, not a defect fix. It carries its own settability matrix, and the ordinary
consequence questions apply to every field it adds — who READS, WRITES, DECIDES, and what VIEW
projects it.

### Ruling — P4.11 read-back scope (ruled 2026-09-09, before compiling batch 1)

Three questions the row leaves open, ruled here because a delegated executor would otherwise
answer them by guessing and the answers are not recoverable from the falsifier.

1. **Read source.** `inspect` reports from the source it already reports from — the disk-derived
   registry projection for gates, the `loadExistingFramework` → `toFrameworkCreationData` result
   for framework advanced fields. A second disk read placed beside the registry would be a second
   derivation of one question with no runtime comparison between them, which is the shape
   `feedback_rederivation_not_consumer` names. The falsifier's "matches the YAML on disk" is
   discharged by the round-trip test and the conformance scenario, not by forcing a second medium.
2. **Field set.** Exactly the 13 parameters the two exception groups name — 2 gate, 11 framework.
   Rendering the whole authored document would also close the exceptions and is refused: CLAUDE.md
   §Instruction surface bounds how much resource-authored text a read surface emits, and `inspect`
   returning `judge_prompt` and `template_enhancements` in full widens it for no closure gain.
3. **Vacuity.** Every new assertion sets a NON-DEFAULT value and reads that same value back. The
   exception text at `:291` already rejects the alternative in its own words — an `ok` return is
   "equally true of a create that wrote loader defaults" — so a scenario asserting only success
   would delete the exception without closing the gap it describes.

### Ruling — P4.14 / P4.15 dispositions (ruled 2026-09-11, before compiling batch 4)

**R0 — the quarantine collection moves to layer 0, and this was not optional.** Both rows were
authored as "add sinks" and "add consumers" against the collection where P4.9 left it,
`modules/prompts/quarantine.ts`. That is not reachable by either row's code.
`.dependency-cruiser.cjs` forbids, at `error` severity, a value import from `engine/` or `infra/`
into `modules/` — and P4.15's two loaders are in `engine/`, while P4.14's two consumers are in
`infra/`. So the shared collection is `shared/utils/resource-quarantine.ts`, which is the only
place all five can reach; `shared/utils` already hosts stateful collections of this shape
(`ResourceCache`, `SimpleRegistry`). **Neither row could have been executed as written**, and the
relocation is a third piece of work that belonged to neither (landed `46610bd2`). Three properties
came with the move: the key gains `type` (because `beginRoot` CLEARS, so a gate walk of a root
would otherwise erase that root's prompt records), `category` becomes optional (gates and
frameworks are FLAT layouts and an empty string asserts a directory level that does not exist), and
`isRefused(path)` plus `mergeQuarantineViews(...)` are added for R2/R3 below.

**R1 — `isRefused` is path-keyed, not id-keyed.** Both P4.14 consumers run their own filesystem
walk and hold a path, not a loaded resource. An id-keyed query would make each of them re-derive
the id the way its loader derives it — a third derivation of a question the loader already
answered, and the indexer's own history says what that costs (`identityOf`'s comment: reading `id:`
instead of the path produced a different key for every nested prompt).

**R2 — the index disposition is ABSENT, not marked.** The falsifier allows either. Absent is
chosen: `resource_index` is a projection of the served catalog, the Python hooks read it, and they
hand its ids to `prompt_engine`, which refuses an unloadable id — so a row for a refused file is a
false promise. A marker would oblige every reader, including hooks in other repos, to remember to
check it, which is the same argument `resource-quarantine.ts` already makes for why a separate
collection beats a flag on the catalog entry. A row that exists for a file which has SINCE become
refused is deleted.

**R2a — refused is not removed, and not a parse failure either.** A refused file is still on disk,
so counting it as `removed` claims a validation failure and a deleted directory are the same event.
`syncTools()` already refuses that conflation for script tools in its own words; refusals get a
third disposition in `SyncResult` with its own report line. A later repair then indexes as `added`,
which falls out rather than being special-cased.

**R3 — the change tracker logs NO event, and keeps the key live.** Same shape from the other
direction: dropping a refused path from the baseline list would make the removal sweep report
`removed` for a file that is present. So its cache key stays in `currentKeys` and its hash is NOT
written. The consequence is the property worth testing: a repair of a never-cached file logs
`added`, and a repair of a previously-valid one logs `modified` — the change event fires when the
resource actually enters the catalog, which is what `resource_changes` is supposed to mean.

**R4 — styles and script tools are out of scope and are not silently left.** `QuarantinedResourceType`
deliberately excludes `style` (a member no loader writes to would read as coverage the collection
does not have), and `syncTools()` KEEPS a tool's index row when the tool fails to load. Both are
the same defect class as P4.14 in channels neither row names. Rows, not remarks — see P4.16/P4.17.

### Ruling — batch 5 dispositions (ruled 2026-09-13, before compiling P4.16–P4.21)

Six rows, three file regions, and the questions each row leaves open — ruled here because a
delegated executor would otherwise answer them by guessing and the answers are not recoverable from
the falsifiers. Sequencing is on FILE REGIONS as the preamble requires: P4.16/P4.17/P4.21 all edit
`infra/database/resource-indexer.ts`, and P4.19/P4.20 both edit `gate-lifecycle-processor.ts`, so
the six rows are three nodes and not six.

**R5 — P4.16 splits at the repair boundary, and only the index half is in scope.** The row's
falsifier asks that a refused style be "recorded where the repair surface can reach it". Measured
2026-09-13: **styles have no `resource_manager` surface at all** — P3.1 (`resource_type: 'style'`,
~1,160 lines of lifecycle/discovery/versioning) is still ☐, so the clause names a surface that does
not exist and no work inside P4.16 could satisfy it. Adding `style` to `QuarantinedResourceType`
and having `StyleDefinitionLoader` fill a sink closes the index half AND removes R4's objection to
the member (it is no longer a type nobody writes to), which is what gives P3.1 a collection to read
when it lands. The second clause is rewritten rather than deleted: a row that asks for two rows'
work fails in a way that reads as under-delivery.

**R6 — P4.17 reuses P4.14's `RefusedResource`, and does not invent a parallel shape.** R2a already
supplies the third disposition the `seen.add(compositeId)` comment was missing; the tool path gets
the same one, not a tool-flavoured copy.

**R7 — P4.18 stamps the serving root at the LOADER, never at the report.** The renderer must not
infer which root served an id. A second derivation of a question the loader already answered is the
shape this plan has been bitten by twice — `identityOf`'s own comment records that reading `id:`
instead of the path produced a different key for every nested prompt.

**R8 — P4.19 consults the quarantine only on the `!has(id)` branch.** Mirrors the discipline the
framework processor already states in its own words, so a registered gate is never redirected by a
quarantined namesake. The refusal names the refused file path and the `update` verb.

**R9 — P4.20 records the produced snapshot only, and must not reach for `recordEditResult`'s
bridge.** Read 2026-09-13: `recordEditResult` (`versioning/version-history-service.ts:346`)
computes `bridged` from `latestSnapshotMatches(...priorLiveSnapshot)` and, on a mismatch, **saves a
version of the prior live state**. A quarantined file has no prior live state the snapshot contract
can project — it is by definition the file that failed validation — so handing it one would either
record the broken bytes as a restorable version or throw inside the mutation. The repair records
version 1 of what it produced, with a description saying there was no prior loadable state, placed
as the `commit` step of `ResourceMutationTransaction.run()` and inlined at the call site, because
`validate:mutation-atomicity` reads the record's position lexically. The gate is not to be relaxed
to accept a different shape.

**R10 — P4.21 establishes its own first question, and the reverse defect it uncovered is its own
row.** The row said it was "unmeasured whether any shipped prompt uses the single-file layout".
**Measured 2026-09-13: zero.** `find server/resources/prompts -name '*.yaml' | grep -v '/prompt.yaml$'`
returns one `category.yaml` and five `tool.yaml` files, no prompts — while `loader.ts:292-297` DOES
accept the layout, so the gap is latent for shipped content and live for anything an operator
authors. Measuring it turned up the same disagreement running the other way, which is P4.22.

### Ruling — batch 6 dispositions (ruled 2026-09-13, before dispatching P4.23–P4.26, P4.28, P4.29)

**Executor tier is owner-directed, and half of the direction cannot be delivered.** The owner asked
for `fable` at low effort on the audit and medium on the fixes. The Agent dispatch surface takes a
model and no effort; no definition under `~/.claude/agents` pins one; only a Workflow `agent()` call
accepts `effort` per spawn, and that needs the owner's explicit opt-in. So all three nodes run at
`fable` with inherited effort — recorded here rather than silently substituted.

**R11 — one owner for the indexer's merged refusal view.** Measured: `application.ts` holds
`promptManager` and `gateManager` and no framework or style loader, so the reload path cannot build
the merge startup builds, and must not try. Startup builds the view once and the reload path
consumes that same view, read by reference. A loader whose record can go stale across a reload —
styles have no auxiliary hot-reload config, while gates, frameworks and script tools do — is a
measured finding to report, not something to paper over with a second walk.

**R12 — P4.26 lands before P4.25 and must fail the tree first.** The completeness check's motivating
instance IS P4.25, so a gate that passes today's tree has not been shown to observe the property.
Its self-test gains the dropped-view shape.

**R13 — `hot-reload-resource-sync.test.ts` claims coverage it does not have, and the P4.25 node owns
it.** Its header says it tests "the fullServerRefresh() ResourceIndexer re-sync path added in
application.ts"; every case builds its own `createResourceIndexer` with no `quarantine`, so no case
could reach P4.25. It belongs to the fix rather than the audit: the falsifier goes where the false
claim is, and one file cannot have two editors.

**R14 — nested-prompt identity comes from the loader's rule.** P4.28 grows the shared layout module
rather than adding a third walk rule, and reconciles ids as well as files: the baseline keys a
resource by `entry.name` while `discoverYamlPrompts` qualifies nested ids (`chain/step`), so reaching
the files with mismatched ids would swap an absence for a disagreement.

**R15 — the audit reports and commits nothing.** A test that missed a defect outside its own property
missed it correctly, and deleting on that basis removes coverage nobody re-examined. Each candidate
returns with the property it claims, a mutation it survives, and — for a duplicate — the sibling
that kills the same mutation. Retirement is ruled in the owning session and routed to whichever
node owns the file.

**R16 — P4.27 is ruled from the record and joins batch 6 (ruled 2026-09-13, after the batch
dispatched).** It was held when first measured, because the direction looked like a fresh precedence
call. It is not one. P6.1 (owner-merged, PR #258) made `modules/prompts/prompt-root-loader.ts` the
sole owner of prompt precedence, and its order — bundle → primary → overlays, later wins — puts
overlays highest, which `resource_index` already matches. So the flat-kind loaders move to that
order and the index does not. One consequence must be measured rather than assumed, because it is
the write-side reader of the same order: D8 sends a write to the highest-precedence WRITABLE root, so
once an overlay outranks the primary, a `resource_manager` write that lands in the primary for an id
an overlay also defines may keep being served from the overlay. Prompts have lived with that since
P6.1. Whether the flat kinds' write path tells the operator is the executor's to establish, and a
silent case is a finding.

### Merge with main, 2026-09-15 (`c48c113a`) — what it cost and what it caught

Thirty-four commits of main landed before batch 6 was relaunched, because four of them changed code
these rows own and a worker on the pre-merge base would have built against a tree that no longer
existed. Fourteen files conflicted, twenty-two hunks. The four that were decisions rather than text:

1. **The gate loader was rewritten** to parse-then-validate and lost `validateOnLoad` entirely, so
   P4.15's refusal recording was re-applied onto the new flow, and the `validationRefusal` helper —
   extracted only to keep the old inline block under the cognitive limit — went with that block.
2. **The two same-named `GateDefinitionYaml` types became one schema's input and output sides**,
   the exact hazard P4.18's receipt named. The provenance stamp moved to the OUTPUT side alone:
   declaring it on the schema would make it authorable, and `validate:declared-surface` would then
   require the tool to expose it.
3. **`writeGateFiles` gained `suppliedKeys` ahead of `options`**, so P4.20's repair call was passing
   its commit options as the supplied-key set. Typecheck caught it this time; every writer with that
   shape was checked rather than only the one that failed, and the framework side was already right.
4. **Creating a gate now records version 1**, so a freshly created gate stopped being a zero case for
   P4.20's discrimination control. The zero case moved to a gate never written through the tool,
   which still holds — the assertion was not relaxed.

Both branches had independently added `saveVersion` to `validate:mutation-atomicity`'s
`RECORDING_METHODS` — main for the create path, this branch for the repair path (P4-F26). Neither
knew of the other, which is the sharpest evidence that the original enumeration was complete by
accident rather than by construction.

**Re-measured on the merged tree**, because a merge that deletes debt makes every earlier bar stale:
`lint:ratchet` **3046 errors / 888 warnings** (was 3102 / 976), `typecheck:tests:ratchet` 364,
`validate:all` **61 steps** (was 59), unit **3407** across 256 suites, integration 952 of 954. The
two failures are P4.30 and nothing else.

### Ruling — P4.9 quarantine, security constraints (owner-ruled 2026-09-05)

Mechanism: the loader RECORDS resources that fail validation instead of discarding them, so
`update` resolves through the registry unchanged. The owner asked that security risk be weighed
before building, and it is the right question: quarantine makes the registry deliberately hold
entries that failed validation, while every existing consumer was written assuming registry
membership implies validity. Four constraints bind the implementation, each needing its own
positive control:

1. **Structurally unexecutable, not flag-guarded.** `prompt_engine`, the `prompts/list`
   registration, `resource://` reads and the chain-step resolver all resolve through the registry.
   A boolean every call site must remember to check is the shape P2b removed from `dry_run`; a
   separate collection or a discriminated type consumers cannot read past is the shape that
   replaced it. Prefer the latter.
2. **The instruction surface is the sharp edge.** CLAUDE.md §Instruction surface records that
   `prompts/list` carries every description and `list detail:"full"` returns every
   `systemMessage`, before anyone invokes anything. A quarantined entry should expose id, path and
   error — not the content that failed to load.
3. **Repair writes resolve through `resolveContainedPath`.** A file that failed to load may sit
   anywhere the loader walked; `validate:resource-path-containment` (P1.7) must cover the repair
   path rather than be assumed to inherit.
4. **Id shadowing is an availability question. RULED 2026-09-09 (owner) — a quarantined entry
   never participates in id resolution, and the fallback it causes is announced.**

   Measured before ruling: all three loaders resolve an id as `primary ?? additional[0] ?? …`
   with the bundled tree trailing (`runtime/resource-roots.ts:39-41`), so today a workspace file
   that fails to load is simply absent and the bundled definition answers. **Quarantine done
   naively would REGRESS that** — recording the broken entry puts it back in the resolution chain
   at the winning position, so a typo in a workspace prompt takes the id dark. The row asked for a
   repair path and would have shipped an outage.

   The ruling, in three parts:

   - **Resolution never walks quarantined entries.** They live in a separate collection keyed by
     `(root, path)`, not in the registry map consumers read. Availability is byte-identical to
     today: workspace `foo` broken, bundled `foo` valid → bundled `foo` serves. This is also how
     constraint 1 is satisfied, and it costs nothing extra: a collection consumers cannot read
     past is structurally unexecutable without any call site remembering a flag.
   - **The fallback is announced, because the silence is the other half of the defect.** Today an
     operator edits a workspace prompt, breaks it, and the bundled one answers with no indication
     their edit is inert. `list` and the served response say which root actually supplied the
     definition and why the nearer one was skipped, carrying the id, the path and the load error —
     and, per constraint 2, NOT the content that failed to load.
   - **A valid resource claiming a quarantined id wins unconditionally**, which follows from the
     first part rather than needing its own rule: resolution only ever saw the valid one. Two
     sub-cases, and they differ in what the operator should be told. A write to the SAME path is a
     repair — on a successful reload the quarantine record is deleted, which is the row's whole
     falsifier. A valid resource at a DIFFERENT path claiming the id serves immediately, and the
     quarantine record stays, now reported as shadowed: repairing that file will change what
     serves, and an operator who is not told that will be surprised by their own fix.

   What this does NOT rule, deliberately: whether a repair write may leave the file still invalid.
   That is the existing settability path's question (validate-before-write), not quarantine's, and
   folding it in here would have made this ruling govern a boundary it does not own.

### Findings (P4 batch A)

- **P4-F1 — P4.1 was a strict subset of P4.5, and the plan listed them as peers.** `framework_gates`
  is the first of the eleven advanced fields, distinguished only by `FrameworkDraftValidator`
  hard-requiring it. Executing them as one edit was correct; executing P4.1 alone would have
  declared one member of a set and left ten identical instances of the same defect. A row whose
  subject is one member of an enumeration should name the enumeration.
- **P4-F2 — `gate_type` is a homonym across the tool/loader boundary, and the collision is what
  blocked the field.** Two different fields, one name, on two sides of a mapping. The tool
  parameter has meant `validation|guidance` since before the loader gained its own `gate_type`, so
  neither side reads as wrong in isolation; only the pair does. Ruled: split to P4.10 rather than
  alias, because an alias is a parallel system with a nicer name.
- **P4-F3 — the defect was already documented in prose at the site, as future work.**
  `gate-file-writer.ts` called the settability of these fields "a separate, out-of-scope
  initiative" and described its own supplied-value branch as unreachable. That is an unmarked ☐
  living outside the ledger: it named the gap accurately, expired never, and nothing enumerated
  it. The gate that closes the class (`validate:declared-surface`) is what a comment cannot be.
- **P4-F4 — a declared, routed, never-read parameter reads as implemented.** `format` reached the
  router and stopped. Two separate artifacts had already absorbed it as normal: the plan priced
  P4.6 as "add fields to the JSON projection", and `validate:conformance-coverage` exempted
  `format` under a reason implying a scenario would close it — a scenario would have PASSED and
  proven nothing. Both are corrected. The general shape: an exemption's stated REASON is a claim
  about the code and rots like any other, but nothing re-reads it, because the exemption's
  existence is what gets audited.

reported failure (P5.9), a failed mutation whose first line claimed success (P5.4), a green
105/105 suite writing into the tree it believed it was isolated from (P5.5), and a prompt loader
that dropped invalid prompts and malformed gates behind a single log line (P5.1/P5.11). Each is
now held by a gate rather than by care — a package-tree diff across every jest run, and
`validate:prompts` running the loader's own schema against any root. Both prompt roots load
clean: 39 of 39 bundled, 92 of 92 personal, zero dropped gates.

convention now makes visible, and P5.13 is blocked on P5.12. The gate's own claim is about
honesty, and it holds.

## P6 — hot-reload parity (in flight, 2026-09-01)

Taken over from `fix/hot-reload-resources-root`, a local branch investigating issue #229. That
branch's headline fix was already superseded by `233b2bf2`; what it also found — and what nothing
else in the repo recorded — is that **reload loaded a different root SET than startup**.

**Branch**: `fix/hot-reload-overlay-parity`, based on `df2e9f14`. Commit `f091967d` landed the fix
**on the branch** — it is NOT an ancestor of `main`. P6 reached main as the squash `5ba103cc`
(PR #258). Cite both: `f091967d` is unreachable from HEAD and a reader checking it out gets nothing.
This is the same squash-breaks-patch-equivalence trap the "do not re-derive" list below already
records for `git cherry`; the receipts above tripped it anyway, which is the argument for naming the
landed sha next to the authored one every time.

frameworks and styles at watch scope**, and **P6.4's falsifier was wrong in a way that would have
sent the next executor to build something impossible** (a lint rule over `tests/**`, which ESLint
ignores by configuration). One count also drifted: `prompts/list` reported 113 on 2026-09-01 and
measures **124** at HEAD with `MCP_RESOURCES_PATH` set, **34** bundled-only — and that 34-vs-124
split is itself the positive control proving the additional-roots merge is live rather than
theoretical.

**One residue is filed rather than closed.** The count-based blindness that hid the defect
(`prompts/list` reporting 113 before and after) is asserted against inside P6.2's suite but enforced
nowhere else, so a future reload gate written against catalog size would still read as passing.
That is P6.4.

**Do not re-derive these — they cost real measurement:**

- `git cherry -v` is **useless** in this repository. It marked all 18 settability commits and all 3
  rescue commits `+` (not upstream) when the settability work was fully landed. Squash-merge
  breaks patch-equivalence. Compare CONTENT, or compare against the squash body's commit list.
- Row **P1.5a**'s claim that `feat/settability-parity` "landed on main by fast-forward" is FALSE —
  it landed as the squash of PR #255. The five commit hashes that row names exist on neither main
  nor the branch; they are pre-rebase versions with identical subjects.
- `rescue/local-main-observability-dashboard` is **KEEP, permanently until its refactor lands**.
  `git branch -a --contains 22e6fee9` returns it and nothing else — it is the sole surviving copy
  of work `plans/backlog/dashboard-refactor.md` protects, and the feature is absent from main
  (`SpanPersister`, `telemetry_spans`, `dashboardHtml`, `PipelineWaterfall`: zero hits in `src`).
- `docs/security-review-plan` was deleted (local + remote) 2026-09-01 after verifying **zero
  branch-only prose lines** in both its files against main. PR #246 was closed unmerged, superseded
  by #249. Commit `390dbb45` remains reflog-reachable.

## Phase order

P1 and P2 are independent and may run in either order. P3.1 is independent; P3.2–P3.3 need P2.1
(`unset`) as their instrument, because the handbook forbids hand-editing under `server/prompts/**`.
P4 and P5 are unordered.

**P1 before P2.3** is the one real edge: P2.3 deletes directories under `resources/prompts/`, and
reviewing that diff is meaningless while 83 prompts are invisible to git (the D6 argument, scoped to
the row it actually reaches).
